# Zmiany zrzutu tirules2 — aktualizacja z upstreamu

Porównanie: nasz zrzut z 2026-10-30 (`docs/rules_tirules/`) → repo https://github.com/dangothemango/tirules
(HEAD z 2026-11-22). Porównano treść po normalizacji (bez linków, wcięć, pogrubień i przypisów `<sup>`),
więc to **zmiany merytoryczne**, nie formatowanie. Lista tematów bez zmian (149).

Zmienione tematy: **137**; razem **+1425 / −1063** linii.
`-` = tekst, który był u nas, `+` = nowy tekst z upstreamu (upstream jest nowszy i został wgrany).

## ⚠️ Do zaktualizowania w zasadach/kodzie

Zrzut jest już nadpisany. Zasady (`docs/rules/R<NN>.md`), kod i testy `@ruling` trzeba teraz przejrzeć pod kątem
poniższych różnic — w pierwszej kolejności linie `+` (nowe/zmienione rulingi) i `-` (usunięte lub przeredagowane).
`R_construction` / `R_warfare` to NASZE adaptacje pod Thunder's Edge — zachowane bez zmian; diff poniżej pokazuje,
co upstream zmienił w wersji PoK (do ręcznego przeniesienia, jeśli dotyczy TE).

Sekcja **Przemapowanie ID rulingów** (na końcu pliku) — co zrobiono z testami i co zostało.

### C_agendas

```diff
- If a player draws the Drive the Debate secret objective while resolving Archived Secret, they cannot score it immediately.
+ If a player draws the Drive the Debate secret objective while resolving Archived Secret, they cannot score it immediately, as the timing window to score it has already passed.

+ The “For” outcome of Arms Reduction only affects dreadnoughts and cruisers. Afterward, if a player’s fighters or ground forces in a space area exceed their capacity, the excess units are removed; they are not destroyed.
```

```diff
+ A space dock’s Production value is not affected by whether its planet is exhausted.
```

### R_abilities

```diff
- _[Dane ruling]_ Whenever a game effect uses or grants a unit ability, treat it as if a unit were using or having that unit ability.
- _[Dane ruling]_ This means abilities that reference unit abilities, such as the Argent Commander, combo with these effects. Similarly, effects that shut down unit abilities, such as the Crimson Flagship, will affect these abilities as well.
+ All abilities that occur “when” an event occurs all trigger before any abilities that occur “after” the same event occurs.
+ All abilities that occur “after” an event occurs all trigger before any abilities that occur “before” the next event occurs.
+ Each paragraph on a card is a separate ability, and is resolved independently of the others. For example, a promissory note’s final paragraph instructing the player to return the card is still resolved, even if an earlier paragraph had no effect.
+ An ability that refers to “a player” may be used on the player resolving it. Only an ability that refers to “another player” excludes that player.
- Multiple action cards with the same name cannot be played during a single timing window to affect the same units or game effect. See the action cards page for more details.
+ An ability that has not finished resolving cannot be triggered again within a timing window that it creates.
- Following on from the above example, had Alice declined to resolve “Alice #2”, she could still resolve “Alice #3”, as Cheng resolved “Cheng #2”, breaking the series of consecutive declinations. However, if Alice had instead declined to resolve “Alice #3”, she would be unable to resolve “Alice #4”, as then each player would have consecutively declined to resolve an ability.
+ If there are multiple abilities that players wish to resolve at the same time during the status phase, such as two players each using the Creuss player’s Wormhole Generator faction technology, they are resolved in initiative order.
- All abilities that occur “when” an event occurs all trigger before any abilities that occur “after” the same event occurs.
- All abilities that occur “after” an event occurs all trigger before any abilities that occur “before” the next event occurs.
+ Whenever a game effect uses or grants a unit ability, treat it as if a unit were using or having that unit ability.
+ This means abilities that reference unit abilities, such as the Argent player’s commander, Trrakan Aun Zulok, combo with these effects. Similarly, effects that shut down unit abilities, such as the Rebellion player’s flagship, the Quietus, will affect these abilities as well.
+ Action Phase
```

### R_action_cards

```diff
- A player may choose to reveal any or all of the action cards in their hand, if they so choose.
+ A player may choose to reveal any or all of the action cards in their hand.
- During the action phase, action cards are dealt out in initiative order.
+ During the status phase, action cards are drawn in initiative order, though this rarely matters in practice.
- If a player’s action card is cancelled, usually via a Sabotage action card, then if that player has a second copy of that action card, they may play it.
- A player cannot target themselves with offensive action cards, such as Lucky Shot.
- If an action card targets “any number”, “up to”, or “each” of a particular type of object, that action card may target zero of those objects. As such, these cards may be played even if they would cause no effect.
+ If a player’s action card is canceled, usually via a Sabotage action card, then if that player has a second copy of that action card, they may play it.
+ A player cannot use action cards that destroy units, such as Lucky Shot, against their own units.
+ If an action card targets “each” of a particular type of object, that action card may target zero of those objects. As such, these cards may be played even if they would cause no effect.
- If a player has more than seven action cards, they must discard the excess immediately. They cannot play them to get down to seven, and the Hacan player cannot trade them.
- For notes about specific action cards, see the action card component notes page.
+ Action cards are discarded faceup. Players may look through the action card discard pile.
+ Initiative Order
```

### R_agenda_phase

```diff
- _[Dane ruling]_ A player may exhaust space stations, The Triad, and Oceans to cast votes, using their influence values in the same manner as planets.
+ (Thunder’s Edge) A space station cannot be elected, although its influence can be used to cast votes.
- A player cannot cast zero votes for an outcome. Casting zero votes is the same as abstaining.
- If an agenda outcome affects multiple players, it does so in speaker order.
+ A player may cast votes with space stations, The Triad, and the Deepwrought player’s ocean cards.
+ Players may exhaust planets during the agenda phase for purposes other than casting votes, such as spending resources or using technology specialties. Planets exhausted this way cannot then be used to cast votes.
+ Casting zero votes for an outcome is the same as abstaining. A player that casts zero votes gains no positive or negative effects of voting for that outcome.
+ The speaker decides tied outcomes even if they cannot vote on that agenda, such as when they are the Nekro player or have played a rider, as deciding a tie is not a vote.
+ An agenda’s outcome affects every player, regardless of how they voted or whether they abstained, unless the agenda states otherwise, such as the Mutiny agenda.
+ During the agenda phase, if the outcome of an agenda affects multiple players, it does so in speaker order, starting with the speaker and proceeding clockwise. If an agenda is resolved during the action phase, such as with the Keleres player’s Executive Order technology, it does so in initiative order.
- Only planets ready at the end of the phase. Technologies, leaders, relics, and other cards do not.
- For notes about specific agenda cards, see the agenda card component notes page.
+ Planets are readied at the end of the agenda phase; other exhausted cards, such as technologies and agents, stay exhausted.
+ The Deepwrought player’s ocean cards, space stations and The Triad are also readied at the end of the agenda phase.
+ Agenda Card Notes
+ Exhausted
+ Space Stations
```

### R_blockaded

```diff
+ (Thunder’s Edge) Coexisting units are always blockaded, so they can produce ground forces but cannot produce ships.
- While a player is involved in a space combat in a system, their units in that system are not blockaded, as both players will have ships in the system. Consequently, they may capture their opponent’s units.
- If that player has a unit in another system that their opponent is blockading, they will be unable to capture their opponent’s units.
```

### R_bombardment

```diff
+ (Thunder’s Edge) If a player makes a bombardment roll against a planet that contains coexisting units, that player chooses which units with Bombardment are bombarding which other player’s units, and rolls against each player separately.
+ Only the active player may bombard.
- Mechs may use their Sustain Damage ability to cancel a hit produced by a bombardment roll.
- Only the active player may bombard. Usually, but not always, only the active player will have ships in the system during an invasion.
- For notes about Harrow, see the L1Z1X faction notes page.
- The “0” side of the d10 represents a result of 10.
- A single planet must be chosen for each unit with the Bombardment ability; any hits generated by that unit will be assigned to units on that planet. For example, when a war sun rolls for its Bombardment ability, all three dice must be rolled to produce hits against units on a single planet; they can not be split between two or more planets.
- If an ability allows a unit to roll one or more additional dice, such as the Plasma Scoring technology, the additional dice must be rolled against units on the same planet.
+ A single planet must be chosen for each unit with the Bombardment ability; any hits generated by that unit will be assigned to units on that planet.
+ Mechs may use their Sustain Damage ability to cancel a hit produced by a bombardment roll.
+ The “0” side of the d10 represents a result of 10.
```

### R_capacity

```diff
- If a player moves more fighters and/or ground forces into a system containing another player’s ships, capacity limits must be met before proceeding to the Space Cannon Offense substep or the Space Combat step.
- If a ship with capacity is destroyed during the Space Cannon Offense step, capacity limits must be met before space combat starts.
- Capacity is checked after the winner of a space combat is determined. As such, it is possible to win a space combat with only fighters remaining, before removing those fighters due to lack of capacity.
+ Capacity limits are checked immediately whenever a combat is not in progress.
+ If, after movement, a player has more fighters and ground forces in the active system than their ships there can carry, they must remove the excess before the Space Cannon Offense step.
+ If a ship with capacity is destroyed during the Space Cannon Offense step, its player removes the excess units immediately, after that player’s hits are assigned and before the next player rolls. The excess units do not remain until space combat.
+ Capacity is checked after the winner of a space combat is determined. It is possible to win a space combat with only fighters remaining, before those fighters are removed due to lack of capacity.
+ Units that are removed due to capacity limits are not destroyed. For example, removed Infantry II units do not roll for the Infantry II unit upgrade’s ability.

+ Space Cannon
```

```diff
- A captured unit may only be returned to the player that originally owned it, and not to another player.
```

### R_coexistence

```diff
- A coexisting structure is always blockaded, regardless of what ships, if any, are in the system.
+ If multiple players may choose to coexist on a planet during the same timing window, the active player chooses first; if they decline, the other player may then choose to coexist.
- If only one player has units on a planet that was in coexistence, the coexistence ends, and that player gains control of that planet if they were the coexisting player.
+ Structures without ground forces may coexist on a planet by themselves, and may maintain control of a planet while another player’s ground forces coexist on it.
+ Structure limits apply to each player separately. A planet in coexistence may contain up to two PDS and one space dock belonging to each player.
+ If only one player has units on a planet that was in coexistence, and that player was coexisting, they gain control of that planet.
+ Outside of the Invasion step of a tactical action, this happens immediately, such as when the controlling player moves all of their units off that planet.
+ During an invasion, if the last of the controlling player’s units on a planet are destroyed, such as by Bombardment, control does not pass to the coexisting player until the Establish Control step. The invading player may land ground forces and gain control of the planet, and the coexisting player remains in coexistence.
+ A player who enters coexistence on a planet they control when ground forces are committed to it may still use Space Cannon against those ground forces during the Space Cannon Defense step. They do not lose control of the planet until the Establish Control step, and if they are then the only player with units on the planet, they are no longer coexisting and retain control.
+ If multiple players are coexisting on a planet and the player that controls that planet no longer has units on it, control passes to the next coexisting player in the order used to resolve abilities in the current phase: initiative order during the action phase, otherwise clockwise order.
+ All coexisting units are blockaded, regardless of what ships, if any, are in the system. This includes units that are not structures, such as mechs with the Production ability.
+ Coexisting units may use their Production ability to produce ground forces, but cannot produce ships.
+ A unit with Planetary Shield on a planet protects all units on that planet, including those of the controlling player and coexisting players. All PDS on a planet, including coexisting PDS, may use Space Cannon during the Space Cannon Defense step against ground forces committed by the active player.
- A player is considered to control a planet they are coexisting on solely when scoring an objective. For any other game ability or effect, they are not considered to control that planet.
+ When a player ends their coexistence by starting a ground combat, all of their units on that planet, including structures, stop coexisting, even if they do not win the ground combat.
+ When scoring an objective, a coexisting player considers both themselves and the controlling player to control that planet. The player that controls the planet does not count a coexisting player as also controlling it.
+ A player may score public objectives even if they coexist on, but do not control, each planet in their home system.
+ A player must control a planet, not merely coexist on it, to explore it.
+ A player cannot spend another player’s coexisting units to pay the cost of an exploration card, such as Volatile Fuel Source.
+ Blockaded
+ Bombardment
+ Ground Combat
+ Invasion
+ Planetary Shield
+ Production
+ Space Cannon
+ Structures
```

### R_combat

```diff
+ Abilities that trigger “at the start of a combat”, other than those on agents, can only be resolved during a combat in which the player resolving them is participating.
+ A player only wins a combat if a space combat or ground combat occurred. Destroying all opposing ships with the Space Cannon ability, destroying all opposing ground forces with the Bombardment ability, landing ground forces on a planet without opposing ground forces, or the opposing player avoiding the combat with the Naalu player’s Foresight faction ability does not count as winning a combat. A planet containing only another player’s structures does not cause a ground combat.
+ Invasion
```

### R_command_tokens

```diff
+ Each faction has sixteen command and seventeen control tokens included in the game.
+ Command tokens on a player’s command sheet are not in that player’s reinforcements.
+ If an effect instructs a player to spend a command token from their reinforcements, that player must have a command token in their reinforcements to do so. The token remains in their reinforcements, unless the effect places it on the game board.
+ Players do not spend command tokens from their fleet pool unless a game effect specifically allows it.
+ A system is only activated by placing a command token during the Activation step of a tactical action or through the primary ability of the Warfare strategy card. Command tokens placed by other effects, such as the primary ability of the Diplomacy strategy card or a retreat, and action cards that target a system, do not activate that system.
```

### R_commodities

```diff
+ For each space station a player controls, their commodity value is increased by one.
+ A player may exhaust a space station at any time to convert any commodities that they have into trade goods.
```

### R_component_action

```diff
+ There is no limit to how many component actions a player may perform during a round, other than any limit stated on the component itself. Each component action uses that player’s action for the turn.
```

### R_deals

```diff
- In general, only the terms of the deal that a player can immediately perform, even without the deal taking place, are binding. For example:
- If a player has four resources available during the Production step of a tactical action, another player may give them four trade goods on the condition that they produce a dreadnought during this Production step. As the active player could produce a dreadnought with the resources, this deal is binding.
- If instead the active player has zero resources available, the same deal would be non–binding.
- The following are further examples of non–binding deals:
+ In general, only the terms of the deal that a player can immediately perform, even without the deal taking place, are binding.
+ The following are examples of non–binding deals:
+ The following are examples of binding deals:
+ Making a deal to use a promissory note in a specific way, when the player who would resolve it already has that promissory note and can resolve it immediately.
+ Making a deal to not make use of a unit’s Space Cannon ability, at the moment that player decides whether to use it.
+ Making a deal to give trade goods to the active player in exchange for the active player choosing that player to replenish their commodities without spending a command token, during the resolution of the primary ability of the Trade strategic action.
+ Making a deal for a player to vote on an agenda in a certain way, when that player is about to cast their votes.
```

```diff

+ If a player has a fighter token or infantry token that is not accompanied by a plastic piece of the same type and cannot replace it, the token is removed instead of destroyed.
+ Units destroyed by space cannon or bombardment rolls are not destroyed during combat. Units destroyed by anti–fighter barrage rolls are destroyed during combat.
```

```diff

+ If a ship moves through or out of multiple gravity rifts, each gravity rift provides +1 to its move value and requires a separate roll for removal. If it moves out of the same gravity rift more than once, that gravity rift provides +1 only once, but each instance requires a separate roll.
- A ship that is removed by a gravity rift will not count toward the fleet limit in the destination system.
+ Each ship rolls for gravity rift removal as it exits the gravity rift, during movement. The fleet pool limit in the active system is checked only after all moving ships arrive, so ships removed by a gravity rift do not count against it.
+ Some effects allow ships to move out of or through a gravity rift without rolling for removal, such as the Circlet of the Void relic and the Nav Suite action card.
+ If a ship with capacity is removed by a gravity rift, any units it would have picked up later in its movement, after exiting the gravity rift, remain where they are.
- If a ship moves through or out of multiple gravity rifts, and/or the same gravity rift multiple times, each instance will provide a +1 to movement, and requires a separate roll for removal.
- If a ship with capacity would transport another unit after moving out of or through a gravity rift, but is removed, that unit remains where it is.
- A system containing a Vuil’raith Dimensional Tear is a gravity rift, and thus an anomaly.
- A future rule change will place the ship movement restrictions of anomalies upon all units.
+ A system containing a Vuil’raith Dimensional Tear is an anomaly.
+ The movement restrictions of anomalies are intended to apply to all units, not only ships.
+ Destroyed
```

### R_movement

```diff
- When an effect moves a unit to an adjacent system, modifying that unit’s move value will have no effect.

However, the transport rules prevent a player’s units from being transported from systems containing one of that player’s command tokens, other than the active system. An ability that moves a player’s ship must also explicitly allow it to transport units from non–active systems containing that player’s faction’s command token for it to be able to do so.
+ When an effect, such as retreating, moves a unit to an adjacent system, modifying that unit’s move value will have no effect.

+ Fighters alone will block other players’ ships from moving through that system, whether they are accompanied by a Space Dock or they are Fighters II.
+ Another player’s ground forces and PDS do not prevent a ship from moving through a system.
+ A deal cannot allow a player’s ships to move through a system that contains another player’s ships.
+ When an ability moves ships outside of a tactical action, the movement restrictions of anomalies still apply, unless the ability says otherwise.

+ Deals
```

### R_nebula

```diff
- A player cannot retreat (or similar) into a nebula, since it would not be the active system.
- The +1 modifier for the defender does not modify anti–fighter barrage, bombardment or space cannon rolls.
- The +1 modifier for the defender has no effect during ground combat.
- A future rule change will place the ship movement restrictions of anomalies upon all units.
- A future rule change will allow ships to move into a nebula when it is not the active system.
+ The movement restrictions of anomalies are intended to apply to all units, not only ships.
+ Units are intended to be able to move into a nebula even when it is not the active system, but not through it.
+ Ships can retreat into a nebula, as a nebula is intended to no longer need to be the active system for units to move into it.
+ Movement bonuses, such as those from the Gravity Drive technology or the Flank Speed action card, are added to the move value of 1.
+ The defender’s +1 applies only to combat rolls during space combat; like other combat roll modifiers, it does not modify anti–fighter barrage, bombardment or space cannon rolls.
+ The following effects change how a nebula affects movement:
+ The Empyrean player’s ships are not affected by nebulae when moving, due to their Voidborn faction ability.
+ The owner of the Circlet of the Void relic ignores the movement effects of nebulae.
+ The Nav Suite action card allows ships to move through a nebula, and ships moving out of a nebula keep their normal move value.
+ The Saar player’s agent, Captain Mendosa, is treated as a bonus and overrides the nebula’s move value of 1.
+ If a Vuil’raith Dimensional Tear is in a nebula, its +1 is applied after the nebula sets each ship’s move value to 1.
```

### R_neutral_units

```diff
- Neutral units are placed in some system within The Fracture when it enters play. Several other game effects also place neutral units.
+ Neutral units are placed in some systems within The Fracture when it enters play. Several other game effects also place neutral units.
- Neutral units do not take turns, own technology, or draw action cards, and so on. They cannot retreat from combat.
- When a hit is assigned against a group of neutral units, it is assigned to the unit that is lowest on the neutral unit reference card; generally, this corresponds to the unit with the lowest cost.
- Hits produced against neutral units may only be assigned to eligible unit types, as usual. For example, Anti–Fighter Barrage hits produced against neutral units may only be assigned to neutral fighters.
+ Neutral units do not take turns, own technology, or draw action cards, and so on.
+ When a hit is assigned against a group of neutral units, it is assigned to the unit that is lowest on the neutral unit reference card.
+ Neutral units with Sustain Damage use it to cancel hits before any remaining hits are assigned. For example, if a neutral destroyer and a neutral dreadnought take one hit, the dreadnought uses Sustain Damage to cancel it.
+ Neutral units cannot make decisions. If a decision must be made for a neutral unit, the speaker makes it.
+ During combat, hits against neutral units are always assigned in the order on the neutral unit reference card, so the speaker does not choose. The speaker does choose which neutral units are hit outside of combat, such as by Space Cannon.
+ If there are no neutral units of a given type left in the supply, and one would be placed, it is not placed.
+ Speaker
+ Sustain Damage
```

### R_objective_cards

```diff
- A status phase objective must have its condition met during the status phase; any activity during the action phase is irrelevant.
- Any number of players may score a given public objective during the status phase. Players are not blocked from scoring an objective if another player earlier in initiative order scores it first, unless the earlier player achieved victory and ended the game by doing so.
+ A status phase objective must have its condition met during the status phase.
+ Any number of players may score a given public objective during the status phase.
+ A player may score an action phase secret objective when they are not the active player.
+ If two players could each score an action phase objective during the same combat, the player whose condition is met first scores first; for example, a flagship or war sun is destroyed during a combat round, before the combat ends and has a winner. If both conditions are met at the same moment, the active player scores first.
+ The Bombardment and Space Cannon steps happen outside of combat.
+ The Anti–Fighter Barrage step, however, is a step during combat.
+ A player with three scored secret objectives may draw a fourth. However, they must immediately shuffle it into the secret objective deck.
- A player may choose to reveal any or all of the secret objective cards in their hand, if they so choose.
- If multiple ground combats happen on separate planets as part of a single invasion during one action, one secret objective may be scored during each.
- The Bombardment and Space Cannon steps happen outside of combat; any number of secret objectives may be scored during those steps, and do not prevent another secret objective being scored during combat that turn.
- The Anti–Fighter Barrage step, however, is a step during combat.
- A player may score an action phase secret objective when they are not the active player.
- A player with three scored secret objectives may draw a fourth. However, they must immediately shuffle it into the secret objective deck.
- For notes about specific objective cards, see the objective card component notes page.
+ A player may choose to reveal any or all of their unscored secret objectives to other players.
+ Active Player
+ Objective Card Notes
```

### R_space_combat

```diff
+ Abilities that resolve at the start of a space combat, such as the Assault Cannon technology and the Mentak player’s Ambush faction ability, are resolved before the Anti–Fighter Barrage step.
+ Abilities that trigger “at the start of a combat”, other than those on agents, can only be resolved during a combat in which the player resolving them is participating.
- A player may retreat into an asteroid field only if that player owns the Antimass Deflectors technology.
- A player may retreat into a supernova only if that player owns the Magmus Reactor Muaat factional technology.
- A player cannot retreat into a nebula.
- The Empyrean player may retreat into a nebula.
- The Shared Research law will not allow a player to retreat into a nebula.
- A player may attempt to retreat from a combat in a gravity rift, but will still be affected by it. See the gravity rift page for more details.

+ Capacity is checked after the winner of a space combat is determined.
+ It is possible to win a space combat with only fighters remaining, before those fighters are removed due to lack of capacity.
+ The following apply to retreats:
+ Ships that have announced a retreat still roll dice during the Roll Dice step of that combat round, then retreat during the Retreat step. Retreating this way avoids abilities that resolve after the combat round, such as the N’orr player’s Exotrireme II.
+ If a space combat reaches a state where neither player can win, the attacker must retreat. If the attacker cannot retreat, their units in that combat are destroyed.
+ Modifying the move value of a ship will have no effect when retreating. Retreats are limited to adjacent systems.
- Modifying the move value of a ship will have no effect when retreating. Retreats are limited to adjacent systems.
- While retreating, a player may transport zero or more ground forces from planets they control in the active system. The player need not decide which fighters and ground forces they transport until they execute the retreat.
- A player cannot leave fighters and ground forces in the space area behind in the active system if they would have unused capacity during the retreat. However, if they have fighters and ground forces in both the space area and on one or more planets in the active system, they may choose which units they transport. If this choice results in all their available capacity being used, they may leave units behind in the space area.
- A player is not required to use all of their available capacity to transport ground forces on planets in the active system.
+ Ships can retreat into a nebula, as a nebula is intended to no longer need to be the active system for units to move into it.
+ A player can retreat into an asteroid field or supernova only if an effect allows their ships to move into it, such as the Antimass Deflectors technology or the Muaat player’s Magmus Reactor technology.
+ Ships retreating out of a gravity rift are affected by it; see Gravity Rift.
+ The Nav Suite action card will not allow a player to ignore an anomaly while retreating, as a retreat does not happen during the Movement step.
+ Ships can retreat out of the active system, and transport units out of it, even if it contains the retreating player’s command token.
+ While retreating, a player may transport zero or more ground forces from planets they control in the active system. The player need not decide which ground forces they transport until they execute the retreat.
- Capacity is checked after the winner of a space combat is determined. As such, it is possible to win a space combat with only fighters remaining, before removing those fighters due to lack of capacity.
+ When a player retreats, their fighters that remain in the space area of the active system are removed, even if that system contains one of their space docks.
+ Ground forces transported by retreating ships, or moved with the Skilled Retreat action card, cannot land on planets in the system they move to; they remain in the space area.
+ A player only wins a combat if a space combat or ground combat occurred. Destroying all opposing ships with the Space Cannon ability, destroying all opposing ground forces with the Bombardment ability, landing ground forces on a planet without opposing ground forces, or the opposing player avoiding the combat with the Naalu player’s Foresight faction ability does not count as winning a combat. A planet containing only another player’s structures does not cause a ground combat.
+ Transport
```

### R_space_stations

```diff
- Several systems contain space stations, which are mechanically similar to planets.
- A player gains control of a space station when they are the only player with units in that space station’s system.
+ Several systems contain space stations.
+ A player gains control of a space station when they are the only player with ships in that space station’s system.
- If the player who controls the space station moves their ships out of the system, they retain control of that space station until another player moves ships in.
+ If the player who controls the space station moves their ships out of the system, they retain control of that space station.
+ A player does not lose control of a space station if a neutral ship is placed in its system, as there is no neutral player.
+ A player whose ships are in a space station’s system with only neutral units gains control of that space station before resolving combat against those neutral units, as there is no neutral player.
- Structures and ground forces cannot be committed to or placed on a space station.
- _[Dane ruling]_ Space stations do count as planets for the purpose of voting.
- Space Stations do not count as planets for the purpose of scoring objectives, and a player may still score public objectives even if another player controls a space station in their home system.
+ Space stations are also readied at the end of the agenda phase.
+ A player may exhaust a space station at any time to convert any commodities that they have into trade goods.
- If a player loses control of a space station, and they now have more commodities than their newly decreased commodity value, they must remove the excess and return them to the supply.
- Players who resolve transactions in this manner need not be neighbors. However, the space stations will not make those players neighbors for other game effects.
- Space stations do not allow players to perform more than one transaction with each other player during each turn of the action phase, or during each agenda of the agenda phase.
- A player may exhaust a space station at any time to convert any commodities that they have into trade goods.
- If a player converts commodities in this manner, the resulting trade goods are not treated as being gained for the purpose of triggering other abilities.
+ Players who resolve transactions in this manner need not be neighbors.
+ A player can exhaust space stations to cast votes. However, space stations are not planets, so they cannot be elected.
+ Space stations are not planets, so attachments such as the Nano–Forge relic and the Ul player’s Terraform promissory note cannot be attached to them.
+ A space station is not a planet; controlling one does not prevent a player from being eliminated.
+ Structures and ground forces cannot be committed to or placed on a space station.
+ Space Stations do not count as planets for the purpose of scoring objectives, and a player may still score public objectives even if another player controls a space station in their home system.
```

```diff
+ A ship in the active system may use its Sustain Damage ability to cancel a hit produced by a space cannon roll during the Space Cannon Offense step.
+ A mech committed to a planet may use its Sustain Damage ability to cancel a hit produced during the Space Cannon Defense step by a unit on that planet.
+ Mechs may use their Sustain Damage ability to cancel a hit produced by a bombardment roll.
+ A non–fighter ship cannot use its Sustain Damage ability to cancel a hit produced by an anti–fighter barrage roll, unless an effect such as the Waylay action card makes it eligible to be hit.
+ A unit may only use its Sustain Damage ability once in each timing window, even if it is repaired.
+ If multiple ships use their Sustain Damage ability, a Direct Hit action card may be played for each.
+ When a player uses the Sustain Damage ability of one of their ships, they do not have to decide if another of their ships in the same combat will use its Sustain Damage ability until after their opponent has used or declined to use a Direct Hit.
+ A mech cannot be destroyed by Direct Hit, as it is not a ship.
+ All damaged units are repaired during the status phase, even if a game effect has caused those units to no longer have the Sustain Damage ability.
```

### R_synergy

```diff
- When researching a technology, a player may treat each technology they own and/or each technology speciality on a planet they control that matches one color of their synergy as any color of that synergy.
+ When researching a technology, a player may treat each technology they own and/or each technology specialty on a planet they control that matches one color of their synergy as any color of that synergy.
- These choices are made individually for each technology and/or each technology speciality.
- For example, a player with two propulsion technologies and a propulsion/biotic synergy ability would meet the prerequisites of the Fighter II unit upgrade technology. They do not have to treat their two technologies as both propulsion or both biotic, but may pick and choose for each.
- Each technology and/or each technology speciality can only be one color of technology at any given point in time.
- For example, a player with just one propulsion technology and a propulsion/biotic synergy ability would fail to meet the prerequisites of the Fighter II unit upgrade technology. Their single propulsion technology may be treated as either a propulsion technology or a biotic technology, but not both at the same time.
- A technology and/or each technology speciality may be treated as different colors of technologies at different points in time.
+ These choices are made individually for each technology and/or each technology specialty.
+ Each technology and/or each technology specialty can only be one color of technology at any given point in time.
+ A technology and/or each technology specialty may be treated as different colors of technologies at different points in time.
+ Technology
```

### R_system_tiles

```diff
+ A home system remains its original owner’s home system, even if another player gains control of its planets.
+ Ships
```

### R_tactical_action

```diff
+ (Thunder’s Edge) The primary ability of the Warfare strategy card allows a player to perform a tactical action in a system that already contains one of their command tokens, without placing a command token.
+ An ability that ends a player’s turn ends that turn as soon as it is resolved. No further abilities may be resolved during that same timing window. “End of turn” abilities may still be resolved.
+ Anti–Fighter Barrage
+ Bombardment
+ Ground Combat
+ Space Cannon
+ Transport
```

### R_technology

```diff
- There are four colors of technologies. See your rulebook for images.
+ There are four colors of technologies: blue (propulsion), red (warfare), green (biotic), and yellow (cybernetic).
+ (Thunder’s Edge) A planet with two technology specialties can be exhausted to ignore either or both of the matching prerequisite symbols.
- If a player is instructed to gain a technology, that player does not have to meet the prerequisites, although most game effects that do this will specify what technologies may be gained by that effect.
- If a player is playing a faction with a faction–specific unit upgrade, they cannot research the generic upgrade for that unit type.
- For notes about specific generic technologies, see the technology notes page.
- For notes about specific generic unit upgrades, see the unit notes page.
- For notes about a technologies of a specific faction, see that faction’s notes page.
+ If a player is instructed to gain a technology, that player does not have to meet the prerequisites.
+ If a player is playing a faction with a faction–specific unit upgrade, they cannot research or otherwise gain the generic upgrade for that unit type.
+ An exhausted technology still satisfies prerequisites.
+ The first technology researched with the primary ability of the Technology strategy card may satisfy a prerequisite of the second technology researched with that ability.
+ If an exhausted technology is returned to a player’s technology deck and later researched again, it is gained readied.
+ Command Tokens
+ Initiative Order
+ Resources
+ Strategic Action
+ Strategy Card
```

### R_trade

```diff
- Say, for example, Alice is the active player, and is performing a strategic action with the Trade strategy card. To her left is Bob. To his left is Cheng, then Diego, then Esther, then Fahd. The full order of steps is:
+ Say, for example, Alice is the active player, and is performing a strategic action with the Trade strategy card. To her left is Bob, then Cheng, then Diego, then Esther, then Fahd. The full order of steps is:
- Alice chooses players for the secondary.
- Bob may/must replenish his commodities by performing the secondary ability.
- Cheng may/must replenish her commodities by performing the secondary ability.
- Diego may/must replenish his commodities by performing the secondary ability.
- Esther may/must replenish her commodities by performing the secondary ability.
- Fahd may/must replenish his commodities by performing the secondary ability.
- A deal made during a given step is non–binding if any part of it is to resolve during a later step. As such, if Alice makes a deal to chose a player with the primary ability in exchange for commodities from that player after they perform the secondary, then such a deal will be non–binding.
+ Alice chooses players for the secondary ability.
+ If Alice chose Bob, Bob must resolve the secondary ability, replenishing his commodities. Otherwise, Bob may resolve it by spending a command token from his strategy pool.
+ Cheng, Diego, Esther and Fahd then do the same, in that order.
+ Choosing players to resolve the secondary ability of Trade without spending a command token is not a transaction. Those players do not need to be neighbors of the active player, and it does not count toward the active player’s transaction limit.
+ A deal made during a given step is non–binding if any part of it is to resolve during a later step. As such, if Alice makes a deal to choose a player with the primary ability in exchange for commodities from that player after they perform the secondary, then such a deal will be non–binding.
+ Neighbors
+ Transactions
```

### R_transactions

```diff
+ Players cannot resolve transactions during the strategy phase or the status phase. Players may still negotiate during these phases, but the terms of a deal that involves trade goods cannot be resolved immediately, so that deal is not binding.
+ The Nomad player’s Temporal Command Suite technology and the Yssaril player’s Deepgloom Executable breakthrough may allow a transaction at these times.
- Captured units may be returned to the player that originally owned them as part of a transaction.
+ Captured non–fighter ships and mechs may be returned to the player that originally owned them as part of a transaction.
+ The Nomad player’s Temporal Command Suite technology may allow a second transaction with the same player in one turn.
+ Commodities received in a transaction cannot be given back as part of that same transaction; doing so would require a second transaction.
+ Deals
- Deals
+ Relics
+ Status Phase
+ Strategy Phase
```

### R_transport

```diff
- A ship that can both move and be transported (i.e. Fighter II) cannot do both by “meeting” a ship with capacity partway through a tactical action movement, as all movement is simultaneous.
- A ship may transport as it retreats or is otherwise moved out of the active system, as the units it transports will be picked up from the active system.
- A ship may pick up ground forces from a planet in the active system, usually to invade a different planet in the same system.
- A ship cannot pick up more units from a planet than it has capacity. However, a ship may pick up any units (including none) in a system as it transports, even if doing so would cause units to be removed due to capacity limits.
- For example, say a carrier (with capacity 4) is in the space area of a system along with four fighters. On the only planet in that system are eight infantry. There are no other units in the system. When the carrier moves out of the system, it obeys the following:
+ A ship cannot pick up more units from a planet than it has capacity.
+ For example, say a carrier (with capacity four) is in the space area of a system along with four fighters. On the only planet in that system are eight infantry. There are no other units in the system. When the carrier moves out of the system, it obeys the following:
- A future rule change will allow a player’s ships to transport units from systems that contain that player’s command token when those ships move outside of a tactical action.
+ A ship may pick up ground forces from a planet in the active system, usually to invade a different planet in the same system.
+ A ship may transport units in its system as it retreats or is otherwise moved out of the active system.
+ A player’s ships are intended to be able to transport units from systems that contain that player’s command token when those ships move outside of a tactical action.
+ A ship that can both move and be transported (i.e. Fighter II) cannot do both by “meeting” a ship with capacity partway through a tactical action movement, as all movement is simultaneous.
+ Invasion
+ Tactical Action
```

### R_unit_upgrades

```diff
- For notes about specific generic unit upgrades, see the unit component notes page.
- For notes about a unit upgrade technology of a specific faction, see that faction’s notes.
```

### R_units

```diff
- If an effect allows a player produce a ship of a specific type, then factional variations do not prevent that player from producing that type of ship.
+ A faction–specific unit counts as its base unit type for all purposes. For example, the Muaat player’s Prototype War Sun is a war sun. Only the researched unit upgrade card is a technology; the unit printed on a faction sheet is not.
+ If an effect allows a player to produce a ship of a specific type, then factional variations do not prevent that player from producing that type of ship.
+ A player cannot remove their own units from the game board at will; units are only removed by game effects, such as being destroyed or captured. The exception is that a player may remove a unit from the board in order to place it when there are none of that type in their reinforcements.
```

### R_victory_points

```diff
+ During the action phase, if multiple players gain victory points at the same time, they gain them in initiative order, starting with the player who has the lowest initiative number, regardless of who the active player is.
+ During the agenda phase, if multiple players gain victory points at the same time, they gain them in speaker order, starting with the speaker and proceeding clockwise.
+ A player can lose victory points. For example, a player who has another player’s Support for the Throne promissory note in their play area loses one victory point when it is returned.
```

### R_warfare ⚠️ lokalna adaptacja TE — NIE nadpisano

```diff
- > ⚠️ LOKALNA ADAPTACJA DO THUNDER'S EDGE (2026-11). tirules2.com opisuje wciąż wersję PoK
- > tej karty (zdjęcie żetonu + redystrybucja). W grze obowiązuje wersja te6warfare
- > (Thunder's Edge), dlatego ten plik został PRZEPISANY pod TE. Teksty karty:
- > assets/data/strategy_cards/te.json. Potwierdzenia: TI Wiki (Warfare, Updated/Notes) oraz
- > komunikat BoardGameArena o rulingu wydawcy (Nullification Field / Minister of Peace).
- > Linie z ⚠️ ZAŁOŻENIE nie mają potwierdzenia — do decyzji użytkownika.
- > Poprzednia (PoK) treść: historia git tego pliku.
- The Warfare strategy card allows a player to perform a tactical action without spending a command token, and to redistribute their command tokens around it. This card’s initiative value is “6”.
- During the action phase, if the active player has the Warfare strategy card, they can perform a strategic action to resolve that card’s primary ability.
- To resolve the primary ability on the Warfare strategy card, the active player performs a tactical action in any system without placing a command token, even if the system already has their command token in it; that system still counts as being activated.
- The active player may redistribute their command tokens before and after this action.
- After the active player resolves the primary ability of the Warfare strategy card, each other player, beginning with the player to the left of the active player and proceeding clockwise, may spend one command token from their strategy pool to use the Production abilities of the units in their home system.
- The spent command token is not placed in their home system; it returns to their reinforcements.
+ The Warfare strategy card allows a player to perform a tactical action without placing a command token, and to redistribute their command tokens between their tactic, fleet, and strategy pools. This card’s initiative value is “6”. During the action phase, if the active player has the Warfare strategy card, they can perform a strategic action to resolve that card’s primary ability.
+ To resolve the primary ability on the Warfare strategy card, the active player performs the following:
+ The active player performs a tactical action in any system without placing a command token, even if the system already has their command token in it; that system still counts as being activated.
+ The active player may redistribute their command tokens before and after this tactical action.
+ After the active player resolves the primary ability of the Warfare strategy card, each other player, beginning with the player to the left of the active player and proceeding clockwise, may spend one command token from their strategy pool to resolve the Production abilities of units in their home system.
+ The command token is not placed in their home system.
- The secondary ability allows ALL units in the home system to use their Production ability (not only space docks, as in the PoK version).
- Ruling wydawcy (BGA, dot. Nullification Field / Minister of Peace): jeśli inny gracz przerwie turę w trakcie taktycznej akcji z Warfare, kończy się tylko ta akcja taktyczna; Warfare i tak rozstrzyga się do końca (redystrybucja żetonów, secondary innych graczy, wyczerpanie karty), a w reszcie tury nie ma już okien „po akcji” ani kolejnych akcji.
- ⚠️ ZAŁOŻENIE: akcja taktyczna z Warfare liczy się jako JEDNA akcja strategiczna (nie dodatkowa akcja taktyczna) – zgodnie z R_action_phase („a single action”).
- ⚠️ ZAŁOŻENIE: poza pominięciem kładzenia żetonu akcja taktyczna przebiega normalnie (ruch, walka, inwazja, produkcja), w tym zakaz ruchu statków Z systemów zawierających własny żeton gracza. Karta zmienia tylko aktywację systemu. Do potwierdzenia przez użytkownika.
+ Resolving the secondary ability of Warfare does not activate the player’s home system. It may be resolved even if that system already contains one of their command tokens.
+ If a tactical action that is part of the Warfare primary ability is canceled, or the resolving player’s turn is ended, the rest of the strategy card is still resolved.
+ During the tactical action of the Warfare primary ability, ships may move into a system that contains one of the player’s command tokens, but ships still cannot move out of a system that contains one of the player’s command tokens.
+ No command token is placed in the system where the Warfare primary ability is resolved, so ships in that system, including ones produced there, may move during a later tactical action that round.
+ Space Dock
```

### R_wormhole_nexus

```diff
- As the wormhole nexus becomes active at the end of the Movement step, only units with the Deep Space Cannon ability in systems containing a gamma wormhole will be able to produce hits during the Space Cannon Offense step during that action.
- If the Creuss player moves their Hil Colish flagship into the inactive wormhole nexus, the wormhole nexus will not flip before they move any other ships. Any other ships they wish to move into the wormhole nexus must do so via either a delta wormhole or a gamma wormhole, unless the Creuss player is utilizing the ability of their agent, Emissary Taivra.
- A ship in the wormhole will single–handedly qualify for the Become the Gatekeeper objective.
+ A ship in the wormhole nexus will single–handedly qualify for the Become the Gatekeeper objective.
```

### R_wormholes

```diff
+ (Thunder’s Edge) There are three advanced types of wormhole: delta, gamma, and epsilon.
+ (Thunder’s Edge) The epsilon wormholes are present on The Sorrow system tile and the Crimson Rebellion home system tile.
- A system with a wormhole is not an anomaly.
+ A system containing a wormhole is not an anomaly.
- A player chooses the path of adjacent systems each ship moves through during movement. If two systems on that path are adjacent because of a wormhole, that ship moves through that wormhole.
+ A player chooses the path of adjacent systems each ship moves through during movement.
- For additional information on how the Creuss interact with wormholes, see their faction notes page.
```

## Przemapowanie ID rulingów (2026-11-24)

- **314** ID w `@ruling(...)` i `tests/rulings_status.json` przepisano na nowe numery (ten sam ruling, inna pozycja lub drobna redakcja; dopasowanie po tekście, pasmo niepewne oceniane ręcznie).
- **105** starych ID nie ma odpowiednika (ruling usunięty upstream albo zmieniony co do treści) — usunięto je z dekoratorów i statusów.
- Sekcje wariantów zastąpionych (`X` → `X-replaced-by-omega`, `X-omega` → `X-omega-replaced-by-omega-omega`) — statusy `na` z wildcardem przeniesione; nagłówki „This card is replaced by the Ω version” dostały status `na`.
- `tests/rulings_debt.json`: usunięto 11 sekcji kart, które straciły rulingi upstream (Blitz, Cripple Defenses, …, Cosmic Phenomenae po poprawce literówki) — `GENERAL_DEBT_LIMIT` 210 → 199.

### Nowe/zmienione rulingi bez testu (211) — strażnik `test_ruling_coverage.py` czerwony

Każdy wymaga testu z `@ruling("<ID>")` albo statusu z powodem (`gap`/`na`/`requires`).


#### C_action_cards

- `C_action_cards/insider-information/2` — If the *Covert Legislation* agenda is revealed, it resolves before *Insider Information*; the player looks at the agenda after the one drawn by the speaker.
- `C_action_cards/lie-in-wait/1` — If players make a deal for one of them to play an action card, *Lie In Wait* resolves after that action card is played and resolved.
- `C_action_cards/scramble-frequency/1` — *Scramble Frequency* rerolls all of the dice rolled for that ability, including dice rolled against different planets.
- `C_action_cards/shields-holding/2` — During a space combat, each player may play a *Shields Holding* during the same timing window, as each card cancels a different set of hits.

#### C_agendas

- `C_agendas/armed-forces-standardization/1` — If the elected player is the Mahact player, they choose which command tokens to remove from their fleet pool. Any other players’ command tokens removed are returned to those players’ reinforcements.
- `C_agendas/arms-reduction/1` — The “For” outcome of Arms Reduction only affects dreadnoughts and cruisers. Afterward, if a player’s fighters or ground forces in a space area exceed their capacity, the excess units are removed; they
- `C_agendas/articles-of-war/1` — Mechs lose all of their printed abilities other than `Sustain Damage`, including `Bombardment`, `Space Cannon` and `Production`. Their cost and combat values are unchanged, including any additional di
- `C_agendas/checks-and-balances/1` — Players choose strategy cards in the normal order, but each player gives the card they choose to another player who does not yet have their full number of strategy cards. A player keeps the card they 
- `C_agendas/checks-and-balances/2` — If the “For” outcome is resolved, a player who has been given a strategy card still chooses a strategy card on their turn and gives it to an eligible player.
- `C_agendas/checks-and-balances/3` — If the “Against” outcome is resolved, it applies to every player, regardless of how they voted; each player readies only three of their exhausted planets, and any planets that were not exhausted remai
- `C_agendas/colonial-redistribution/3` — A planet that contains neutral infantry cannot be elected, as players must cast votes for a planet that is controlled by a player.
- `C_agendas/committee-formation/3` — The Nekro player may own and resolve Committee Formation, even though they cannot vote.
- `C_agendas/enforced-travel-ban/4` — When the *Enforced Travel Ban* law is in play, the `Space Cannon` ability may still be used through wormholes.
- `C_agendas/galactic-crisis-pact/1` — If *Leadership* is elected, players may still spend influence to gain additional command tokens when resolving its secondary ability.
- `C_agendas/minister-of-commerce/2` — The owner of Minister of Commerce gains trade goods from Minister of Commerce when they replenish commodities, even if they already have their maximum number of commodities.
- `C_agendas/minister-of-war/1` — Performing any action is the trigger for the remainder of the ability of *Minister of War*.
- `C_agendas/minister-of-war/2` — Resolving Minister of War is not an action. As such, a player with the *Fleet Logistics* technology may perform three actions in one turn.
- `C_agendas/political-censure/2` — If *Political Censure* is elected by *Miscount Disclosed*, the previous owner loses one victory point when they lose the card, and the newly elected player gains one victory point. If the same player 
- `C_agendas/political-censure/2.1` — While *Political Censure* is being voted on again, it remains in play, so its current owner still cannot play action cards until the vote has resolved.
- `C_agendas/political-censure/3` — *Political Censure* only prevents the elected player from playing action cards; it does not affect strategy cards. The elected player cannot discard *Political Censure* themselves.
- `C_agendas/public-execution/1` — The Yssaril player’s Crafty faction ability does not prevent them from discarding their action cards if they are elected by Public Execution, as the discard is not caused by the hand limit.
- `C_agendas/public-execution/2` — If *Public Execution* is resolved during the action phase, such as with the Keleres *Executive Order* technology, the elected player may still vote in the following agenda phase.
- `C_agendas/publicize-weapon-schematics/4` — If the Nekro player has copied a faction–specific war sun unit upgrade technology with *Valefar Assimilator*, they own a war sun technology for the purposes of *Publicize Weapon Schematics*.
- `C_agendas/shared-research/1` — If the “Against” outcome is resolved, a player with no command tokens in their reinforcements does not place one. They do not take a command token from their command sheet.*
- `C_agendas/shared-research/2` — If the “Against” outcome is resolved, a player places the command token in their home system even if they control no planets there.*
- `C_agendas/swords-to-plowshares/1` — If a planet contains only one infantry, that infantry is destroyed and its owner gains one trade good. All trade goods from Swords to Plowshares are gained together, so the Mentak player may use their
- `C_agendas/swords-to-plowshares/3` — Neutral infantry are unaffected by *Swords to Plowshares*, as there is no neutral player.*
- `C_agendas/wormhole-reconstruction/2.1` — (Sorry, Creuss.)

#### C_exploration_cards

- `C_exploration_cards/demilitarized-zone/1` — The Xxcha player may use their `Peace Accords` faction ability to gain control of a planet with *Demilitarized Zone* attached.
- `C_exploration_cards/demilitarized-zone/2` — Ground forces cannot be committed to a planet with *Demilitarized Zone* attached.*
- `C_exploration_cards/ion-storm/5` — If the Ion Storm token would be placed in an entropic scar, the *Ion Storm* card is still placed in the common play area, and the token is discarded.
- `C_exploration_cards/mirage/1` — The player that gains the Mirage planet card gains control of Mirage and places a control token on it. As it was not controlled by another player, they explore it as a cultural planet.

#### C_promissory_notes

- `C_promissory_notes/support-for-the-throne/4` — Activating a system that contains only another player’s control tokens does not cause their *Support For The Throne* to be returned, as control tokens are not units.
- `C_promissory_notes/support-for-the-throne/6` — A player who has given away their *Support For The Throne* may activate systems containing the holder’s units without penalty.
- `C_promissory_notes/support-for-the-throne/8` — A player may give their *Support For The Throne* to another player even if doing so gives that player the victory point they need to win the game.
- `C_promissory_notes/support-for-the-throne/8.1` — Doing so is a betrayal most foul.*

#### C_relics

- `C_relics/dominus-orb/1` — *Dominus Orb* does not increase the distance units may move; they must still be able to reach the active system.
- `C_relics/maw-of-worlds/2` — The planets exhausted by *Maw of Worlds* will ready at the end of the agenda phase.
- `C_relics/metali-void-shielding/5` — If the Nekro player owns *Metali Void Shielding*, their ground forces participating in space combat due to their flagship, *The Alastor*, may be damaged.
- `C_relics/scepter-of-emelpar/3` — A command token spent from reinforcements using *Scepter of Emelpar* instead of from the strategy pool counts toward objectives that require spending command tokens, such as *Galvanize the People*.
- `C_relics/the-silver-flame/1` — The Saar player is unaffected by the negative effect of *The Silver Flame*; if their home system is purged, they may still score public objectives due to their `Nomadic` faction ability.
- `C_relics/the-silver-flame/2` — Using *The Silver Flame* always causes The Fracture to enter play, regardless of the result of the roll.
- `C_relics/the-triad/3` — *The Triad* may be exhausted to cast votes.
- `C_relics/the-triad/4` — For *The Triad*, an Unknown Relic Fragment counts as a fourth type of relic fragment, in addition to Hazardous, Cultural, and Industrial.
- `C_relics/the-triad/5` — *The Triad* is not exhausted by the *Maw of Worlds* relic.
- `C_relics/the-triad/6` — The Saar player does not gain a trade good from their `Scavenge` faction ability when they gain *The Triad*.

#### C_technology

- `C_technology/antimass-deflectors/2` — If a player gains then loses the *Antimass Deflectors* technology, their units may remain in and move out of any asteroid fields they are currently in, but cannot move additional units into or through
- `C_technology/fleet-logistics/5` — If a player’s first action is a strategic action, the other players resolve the secondary ability of that strategy card before the player performs their second action.
- `C_technology/graviton-laser-system/1` — *Graviton Laser System* has no effect during the **Space Cannon Defense** step, as hits can only be assigned to ground forces.
- `C_technology/graviton-laser-system/2` — Hits produced while *Graviton Laser System* is in effect may still be canceled by the `Sustain Damage` ability.
- `C_technology/graviton-laser-system/3` — *Graviton Laser System* applies to every die rolled for the `Space Cannon` roll, including dice rolled by units using another unit’s `Space Cannon`, such as the Ral Nel player’s Linkships.
- `C_technology/gravity-drive/1` — The +1 from *Gravity Drive* may be combined with the +1 from the *Flank Speed* action card, applying +2 to the move value of that ship.
- `C_technology/hyper-metabolism/1` — The text of *Hyper Metabolism* is changed to: “During the status phase, gain 3 command tokens instead of 2.”
- `C_technology/integrated-economy/3` — A player explores a newly controlled planet before resolving *Integrated Economy*, so any attachment gained from that exploration affects the planet’s resource value.
- `C_technology/integrated-economy/5` — No unit is using its `Production` ability. The *Sarween Tools* technology and similar effects will not apply.
- `C_technology/integrated-economy/6` — Effects that let a player produce a unit for less than its cost, such as the Nomad player’s commander, Navarch Feng, do not change that unit’s cost.*
- `C_technology/light-wave-deflector/1` — A ship that uses *Light/Wave Deflector* to move through a system containing another player’s ships may pick up and transport units from that system.
- `C_technology/light-wave-deflector/2` — *Light/Wave Deflector* does not allow a player to produce ships in a system that contains another player’s ships.
- `C_technology/neural-motivator/1.1` — The *Politics* strategy card.
- `C_technology/neural-motivator/1.2` — The *Minister of Policy* law.
- `C_technology/neural-motivator/1.3` — The *Functioning Base* exploration card.
- `C_technology/neural-motivator/1.4` — The *Prophet’s Tears* relic.
- `C_technology/plasma-scoring/2` — If a player makes separate `Bombardment` rolls against multiple coexisting players on the same planet, *Plasma Scoring* adds one additional die to only one of those rolls.
- `C_technology/plasma-scoring/3` — A space dock using the `Space Cannon` ability due to the *Experimental Battlestation* action card may roll an additional die from *Plasma Scoring*.
- `C_technology/x89-bacterial-weapon-omega-omega/5` — A player who bombards another player’s coexisting units on a planet they control must still exhaust that planet.

- `F_arborec/dirzuga-rophal/6` — A unit with `Production` in an entropic scar still has `Production`, even though it cannot use it. As such, Dirzuga Rophal may still be used in that system.

- `F_argent/zeal/1.1` — When the Argent player is the speaker, effects timed “after the speaker votes”, such as the *Bribery* action card, resolve right after the Argent player votes.

- `F_hacan/quantum-datahub-node/4` — The *Public Disgrace* action card cannot be played against a strategy card the Hacan player takes with *Quantum Datahub Node*, as *Quantum Datahub Node* is resolved at the end of the strategy phase an

- `F_jol_nar/brilliant/2` — When the Jol–Nar player uses `Brilliant`, they may research a second technology by spending six resources, without spending an additional command token.

- `F_jol_nar/spatial-conduit-cylinder/1` — *Spatial Conduit Cylinder* is exhausted after the system is activated, before movement; the active system must already contain one of the Jol–Nar player’s units.

- `F_letnev/gravleash-maneuvers/4` — The value is copied after the *Gravity Drive* technology is applied. A ship that copies another ship’s move value cannot then apply +1 from *Gravity Drive*.

- `F_lizix/the-helmsman-dark-space-navigation/4.1` — Ships do not roll for removal if the L1Z1X player owns the *Circlet of the Void* relic.

- `F_muaat/adjudicator-baal-nova-seed/3.1` — The Avernus token is purged, not returned to the Muaat player. This also purges the Avernus planet card, *The Nucleus* legendary planet ability card, and any attachments on Avernus.

- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-omega/2` — If all of a player’s units in a combat are destroyed, they may still place units with this ability. If they do, combat will continue.
- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-omega/3` — Capacity is not checked during combat. As such a player may use Brother Milor ΩΩ to place fighters into a space combat where they have no spare capacity.
- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-omega/4` — If all units in the combat are destroyed, and there are excess hits that have not been assigned to a unit, those hits will lapse, and cannot be assigned to the newly placed units.*
- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-omega/4.1` — If a ship uses its `Sustain Damage` ability, and is then destroyed by a *Direct Hit* action card, then any remaining hits may be assigned to any placed fighters.*
- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-omega/5` — If the Yin player uses Brother Milor ΩΩ when a unit is destroyed that is both a ground force and a ship due to some game effect, that unit’s owner may place two fighters or two infantry.
- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-omega/5.1` — If placed this way during a space combat, any newly placed infantry cannot participate. If placed this way during a ground combat, any newly placed fighters cannot participate.
- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-replaced-by-omega-omega/3` — If all of a player’s units in a combat are destroyed, they may still place units with this ability. If they do, combat will continue.
- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-replaced-by-omega-omega/5.1` — If a ship uses its `Sustain Damage` ability, and is then destroyed by a *Direct Hit* action card, then any remaining hits may be assigned to any placed fighters.*
- `F_yin/brother-milor-omega-replaced-by-omega-omega/6` — If all ships in the system are destroyed by hits produced from a space cannon roll, the player that produced those hits may score the *Turn Their Fleets to Dust* secret objective before the new fighte
- `F_yin/devotion/1` — The text of `Devotion` is changed to: “After each space battle round, you may destroy 1 of your cruisers or destroyers in the active system to produce 1 hit and assign it to 1 of your opponent’s ships
- `F_yin/devotion/4` — If the targeted ship cancels the hit with its `Sustain Damage` ability, the Yin player may play the *Direct Hit* action card to destroy that ship.
- `F_yin/impulse-core/1.1` — The chosen ship must be in the active system.*
- `F_yin/yin-ascendant/1.1` — If the Yin player gains the Mahact alliance card, they gain access to the ability of Il Na Viroset, the Mahact commander.
- `F_yin/yin-ascendant/2` — The Firmament and the Obsidian each have their own alliance card.

#### R_entropic_scars

- `R_entropic_scars/notes/2.2` — A unit in an entropic scar still has its unit abilities, even though it cannot use them. As such, a text ability that only requires a unit with a unit ability may still be used, such as the Arborec pl
- `R_entropic_scars/notes/4.1` — A discarded wormhole token is returned to the supply, or to its owner’s reinforcements if it belongs to a player, such as the Creuss player’s wormhole tokens.*
- `R_entropic_scars/notes/4.2` — The Creuss player may use the *Wormhole Generator* technology to place or move one of their wormhole tokens into an entropic scar; that token is discarded.
- `R_entropic_scars/notes/4.3` — The delta wormhole of the *Hil Colish* is not affected by an entropic scar, as the *Hil Colish* is a ship, not a wormhole token.
- `R_entropic_scars/notes/4.4` — If an exploration card would place a gamma wormhole token in an entropic scar, the card is still purged as part of its resolution, and the token is discarded.
- `R_entropic_scars/notes/4.5` — If the *Ion Storm* exploration card would place its token in an entropic scar, the card is still placed in the common play area, and the token is discarded.

#### R_exploration

- `R_exploration/notes/1` — When a player gains control of a planet that is not controlled by another player, they explore it before resolving any “after you gain control of a planet” abilities, such as the *Integrated Economy* 

#### R_frontier_tokens

- `R_frontier_tokens/notes/2` — If a system tile contains a space station, but no planets, then a frontier token will be placed in that system during game setup.
- `R_frontier_tokens/notes/3` — Frontier tokens are not placed in systems in The Fracture when it is brought into play, as frontier tokens are only placed during setup. However, if The Fracture is in play when the Empyrean player re
- `R_frontier_tokens/notes/4` — If Thunder’s Edge is placed in a system containing a frontier token, that token is not removed.
